size.](https://airporttocitycenter.com/manchester/private-transfer-vs-tram/)\n\n## On arrival at Manchester Airport\n\nFrom the arrivals hall to each way in\n\n![](https://airporttocitycenter.com/icons/mode-train.svg?v=830f4017)\n\n**Finding the station**\n\nThe station sits between Terminals 2 and 3, linked to both by lifts, escalators and moving walkways — about 10 minutes from Terminal 2, 5 from Terminal 3. It has four platforms with departure boards throughout, so check yours before the barriers.\n\n![](https://airporttocitycenter.com/icons/mode-bus.svg?v=ad040488)\n\n**Finding the stop**\n\nBus 43 leaves from stand E at The Station, the shared interchange between Terminals 2 and 3, a 7 to 10 minute walk from either terminal. Check the board there for the next departure — it comes at least every 30 minutes, even overnight.\n\n![](https://airporttocitycenter.com/icons/ticket.svg?v=657f9357)\n\n**Tickets**\n\nBuy your train ticket first — at the machines in baggage reclaim or the rail desk at the station — since the Bee Network card that works on the tram and bus 43 doesn't cover the train. Tap that same card at the tram platform reader or on the bus instead.\n\n![](https://airporttocitycenter.com/icons/mode-taxi.svg?v=6b02dc9a)\n\n**The taxi rank**\n\nThere's a taxi rank outside each terminal and another at The Station — just walk up, no booking needed. The fleet is wheelchair-accessible and drivers take cards with no added fee.\n\n![](https://airporttocitycenter.com/icons/phone.svg?v=139ed4dc)\n\n**Uber and Bolt**\n\nUber and other private-hire pickups use the signed \"Express Pick Up\" point at each terminal — T2 or T3, not the taxi rank. Open your app after you land and it'll route you there.\n\n![](https://airporttocitycenter.com/icons/plane.svg?v=ead080e9)\n\n**Which terminal**\n\nOnly Terminals 2 and 3 take scheduled flights, and each has its own taxi rank and Express Pick Up point — they don't share one. The station for the train, the tram and bus 43 is shared instead, a short walk from either.\n\n## Before you travel\n\nWhere each one starts, how long it runs, what it carries\n\n![](https://airporttocitycenter.com/icons/mode-bus.svg?v=ad040488)\n\n43 · Bee NetworkBus 43\n\nFromThe bus stands at The Station, the interchange between Terminals 2 and 3 (stand E in TfGM's data), a 7- to 10-minute walk from the terminals\n\nToPiccadilly Gardens, via Wythenshawe, Northenden, Withington, Fallowfield, Rusholme, the Royal Infirmary and the universities on Oxford Road\n\nRunsAll night\n\nJourney48–82 min\n\nThe single \"hopper\" ticket is £2.00 adult, £1.00 child; under-fives travel free. Tap a contactless card or phone at the door for the same £2.00, with further Bee Network buses free within an hour and a £5.00 daily cap on the same card. A 1-day Bee Bus ticket is £5.00 (£2.50 child), valid until 03:59 the next morning; a 1-day family ticket is £9.00 for one or two adults with one to three children, off-peak only. Cash is accepted on board.\n\nIt runs all night, every 30 minutes from about 20:00 to 04:35, then every 10 minutes from 09:00 to 18:00 on weekdays (every 15 on Sundays). The night bus takes 48 minutes; by day about 70, up to 82 at 07:10 on a weekday.\n\nIt is £2.60 cheaper than the tram and £4.20 cheaper than the train, taking about the same time as the tram by day and less at night. It is a city bus making 51 stops, the last stretch along Wilmslow Road and Oxford Road, with no luggage rack.\n\nThe terminus is Piccadilly Gardens, the square; the 43 does not enter the railway station's forecourt.\n\nTap once when you board; there is no tap-out on a bus. If you arrived by tram and forgot to tap out there, the bus tap cannot repair it and the tram leg is charged as an incomplete journey.\n\nThe airport lists eight Bee Network routes at The Station — 43, 103, 130, 199, 248, 288, 313 and 368 — and only the 43 goes to the city center.\n\n[Bus 43: what it costs for one to eight travelers](https://airporttocitycenter.com/manchester/bus-43/)\n\n[Check Bee Network bus fares with TfGM](https://tfgm.com/tickets-and-passes/bus-tickets)\n\n![](https://airporttocitycenter.com/icons/mode-train.svg?v=830f4017)\n\ntrain · Northern, TransPennine Express and Transport for WalesTrain to Piccadilly\n\nFromThe railway platforms in The Station, between Terminals 2 and 3 — about 10 minutes' walk from Terminal 2 and 5 from Terminal 3; ticket machines in baggage reclaim and at the station, ticket office 06:30 to 22:30\n\nToManchester Piccadilly, direct; some trains run on to Manchester Oxford Road, Deansgate, Salford Crescent and Manchester Victoria\n\nRuns03:21–02:28\n\nJourney13–20 min\n\nThe Anytime Day Single is £6.20, valid on any train of any operator at any hour on the day shown, bought online in advance, at a ticket office, at a machine, or immediately before travel. An Off-Peak Day Single is £5.20 when off-peak hours apply; check before you travel. Children aged 5 to 15 pay half on most tickets, under-fives travel free on most journeys. Advance Singles from £3.40 exist for one booked train only, valid on booked TfW services only.\n\nThe Bee Network tap does not work on this train — it covers only Greater Manchester's buses and trams. Buy a ticket instead.\n\nIt is £1.60 more than the tram and £4.20 more than the bus, and it saves 36 minutes on the tram and about 50 on the daytime bus. At 16 minutes it is the fastest thing on this page by a distance.\n\nAdvertised \"from\" prices are not the walk-up fare: Northern's £3.90 needs booking 9 weeks ahead, TPE's £5.70 is its cheapest Advance, and Advance fares tie you to one specific train. A flight is not a train, so book the walk-up fare instead.\n\nIt runs through the night on weekdays: trains at 00:38, 01:14, 01:50 and 02:28 on Tuesday to Saturday mornings, then the day's first at 03:21 — the longest gap is 53 minutes. The airport's own page prints different times (first 04:12, last 01:34); go by the timetable's 03:21 and 02:28.\n\nThe gap is the weekend. Saturday's last train is 23:46 and Sunday's first 07:37 (07:20 from 15 November); Sunday night has one train at 00:38 and then nothing until 03:59. The 43 covers both.\n\nA ticket bought to \"Manchester CTLZ\" rather than to Piccadilly includes the Metrolink in zone 1, so St Peter's Square, Deansgate-Castlefield, Market Street and Victoria come free with it.\n\nUp to eight trains an hour by day, unevenly spaced — three operators share the line — so a wait is rarely more than ten minutes. The airport says \"every 10 minutes\"; Northern's \"every 23 minutes\" counts its own trains only.\n\nTuesday to Friday: first train 03:21 (03:59 on Mondays), last 02:28 the next morning, with night trains at 00:38, 01:14 and 01:50 before it. Saturdays: 03:21 to 23:46, then nothing until Sunday's first at 07:37 (07:20 from 15 November). Sundays: to 23:36, then 00:38 on Monday morning.\n\n[Train to Piccadilly: what it costs for one to eight travelers](https://airporttocitycenter.com/manchester/train/)\n\n[Check times and fares with National Rail](https://www.nationalrail.co.uk/journey-planner/?type=single&origin=MIA&destination=MAN&leavingType=departing&leavingDate=150926&leavingHour=10&leavingMin=00&adults=1&extraTime=0)\n\n![](https://airporttocitycenter.com/icons/mode-tram.svg?v=1e5e8ab5)\n\nMetrolink · Transport for Greater Manchester (Bee Network)Metrolink tram\n\nFromThe Metrolink platform inside The Station, between Terminals 2 and 3; tap in at the reader on the platform before boarding\n\nToDeansgate-Castlefield in 49 minutes, St Peter's Square in 52, Market Street in 55 and Victoria in 60, via Wythenshawe, Sale Water Park, Chorlton, Trafford Bar and Cornbrook\n\nRuns05:45–23:15\n\nJourney49–60 min\n\nThe single for all four zones is £4.60, to be finished within two hours; under-16s pay half. Tap in at the platform and out at your stop for the same £4.60. One zone is £1.40, rising with each zone crossed; the city center is zone 1. The day cap on the same card is £4.90 off-peak or £7.10 if you tap in between 07:00 and 09:30 on a weekday, and £31.00 for a week.\n\nIt costs £4.60 because the airport is in zone 4 and the center is zone 1, so the ride crosses every zone and pays the network maximum. One zone is £1.40. There is no airport supplement; there is simply no way to reach town through fewer zones.\n\nTap out. A journey tapped in and never tapped out is charged the full £4.60 and counts towards no cap. Use the same card or phone every time, one card per adult; tap-and-go charges adult fares, so buy family tickets at the platform machines for children.\n\nGoing back the same day by tap costs £4.90 in total, not £9.20: the all-zone off-peak day cap is thirty pence more than one single. It is £7.10 if the first tap is between 07:00 and 09:30 on a weekday.\n\nIt is 2.3 times the price of the 43 for the same 50 minutes, and slower than the train by half an hour. What it buys is a seat and stops the bus and the train do not make: Wythenshawe, Chorlton, Deansgate-Castlefield, St Peter's Square, Victoria.\n\nBuy or tap before boarding; nothing is sold on the tram. Untapped at an inspection is £60 on the spot or a £120 penalty fare.\n\nThe last through tram is 23:15 (00:15 on Fridays and Saturdays) and the first 05:45 (06:45 on Sundays). Outside those hours the 43 is the public transport, and on Tuesday to Saturday mornings the £6.20 train runs until 02:28.\n\nFirst tram 05:45, Monday to Saturday (06:45 on Sundays). The last tram through to the city center leaves at 23:15 (00:15 on Friday and Saturday nights); trams after that, up to 00:15 (01:15 on Fridays and Saturdays), terminate at Firswood, short of the center. The same holds for a group of up to eight. How we weigh price against time is set out on the fares page, in numbers.\n\nWhat is the cheapest way from Manchester airport to Manchester city center?\n\nFor one traveler, the Bus 43 at £2. Per-person fares multiply with the size of your party and per-vehicle fares do not, so check the total for your group rather than the single fare.\n\nHow do I get from Manchester airport to Manchester city center by train?\n\nOne train reaches Piccadilly: the Train to Piccadilly, £6.20 for one traveler, 13–20 min, to Manchester Piccadilly. It is not the cheapest way in: the Bus 43 at £2 is. The fastest way overall is the Pre-booked private transfer, 15 min. [See every option by train](https://airporttocitycenter.com/manchester/by-train/)\n\nHow do I get from Manchester airport to Manchester city center by tram?\n\nOne tram reaches Piccadilly: the Metrolink tram, £4.60 for one traveler, 49–60 min, to Deansgate-Castlefield in 49 minutes. It is not the cheapest way in: the Bus 43 at £2 is. The fastest way overall is the Pre-booked private transfer, 15 min. [See every option by tram](https://airporttocitycenter.com/manchester/by-tram/)\n\nHow do I get from Manchester airport to Manchester city center by bus?\n\nOne bus reaches Piccadilly: the Bus 43, £2 for one traveler, 48–82 min, to Piccadilly Gardens. For one traveler that is the cheapest way into Manchester by any means. The fastest way overall is the Pre-booked private transfer, 15 min. [See every option by bus](https://airporttocitycenter.com/manchester/by-bus/)\n\nHow do I get from Manchester airport to Manchester city center by taxi?\n\nOne taxi reaches Piccadilly: the Pre-booked private transfer, £54 for one traveler, 15 min, to any address. It is not the cheapest way in: the Bus 43 at £2 is. [See every option by taxi](https://airporttocitycenter.com/manchester/by-taxi/)\n\nHow long does it take to get from Manchester airport to Manchester city center?\n\nBus 43: 48–82 min. Metrolink tram: 49–60 min. Train to Piccadilly: 13–20 min. Pre-booked private transfer: 15 min. Manchester Airport is 16 km from Manchester, by road.\n\nHow far is Manchester airport from the city center?\n\n16 km to Manchester, by road; the airport's own \"13 km\" is measured to the same page's \"approximately eight miles\" to Manchester City Centre, point unstated; TransPennine Express gives the same 8 miles (13 km) by rail to Piccadilly, not to the center. The fastest way in takes 15 min; the cheapest, the Bus 43, 48–82 min.\n\nWhere is the train station at Manchester airport?\n\nThe station sits between Terminals 2 and 3, linked to both by lifts, escalators and moving walkways — about 10 minutes from Terminal 2, 5 from Terminal 3. It has four platforms with departure boards throughout, so check yours before the barriers.\n\nIs there Uber or Bolt at Manchester airport?\n\nUber and other private-hire pickups use the signed \"Express Pick Up\" point at each terminal — T2 or T3, not the taxi rank. Open your app after you land and it'll route you there.\n\nWhere do I catch the Bus 43 at Manchester airport?\n\nThe bus stands at The Station, the interchange between Terminals 2 and 3 (stand E in TfGM's data), a 7- to 10-minute walk from the terminals. Follow the signs from arrivals. It leaves you at Piccadilly Gardens, via Wythenshawe, Northenden, Withington, Fallowfield, Rusholme, the Royal Infirmary and the universities on Oxford Road.\n\nIs there a train from Manchester airport to Manchester at night?\n\nThe Train to Piccadilly runs 03:21–02:28. The Metrolink tram runs 05:45–23:15. Outside those hours the bus 43 and the pre-booked private transfer are what remain; the bus 43 is £2 for one traveler. Tuesday to Friday: first train 03:21 (03:59 on Mondays), last 02:28 the next morning, with night trains at 00:38, 01:14 and 01:50 before it. Saturdays: 03:21 to 23:46, then nothing until Sunday's first at 07:37 (07:20 from 15 November). Sundays: to 23:36, then 00:38 on Monday morning.\n\nDoes the Train to Liverpool go to Piccadilly — Piccadilly station for the train, Piccadilly Gardens for the 43, St Peter's Square for the tram?\n\nLiverpool is a separate city, not part of central Manchester.\n\nDoes the Private transfer to Liverpool go to Piccadilly — Piccadilly station for the train, Piccadilly Gardens for the 43, St Peter's Square for the tram?\n\nThis goes to Liverpool, not Manchester: the driver takes you to any address there.\n\n### Worth knowing\n\nThe Station, between Terminals 2 and 3, holds the railway platforms, the Metrolink platform and the bus stands, five to fifteen minutes' walk from the terminals. Bus 43 is £2.00 to Piccadilly Gardens and runs 24 hours: every 10 minutes by day, every 30 at night, taking 48 minutes at night and about 70 by day. The tram is £4.60 to St Peter's Square in 52 minutes, every 15 minutes, last through tram 23:15 (00:15 Fridays and Saturdays). The train is £6.20 and 16 minutes.\n\nThe train to Piccadilly takes 13 to 20 minutes, up to eight an hour. The walk-up Anytime Day Single is £6.20 at any hour, the Off-Peak Day Single £5.20 when off-peak hours apply, both sold at the machines in baggage reclaim and at the station. Advertised \"from\" prices (Northern £3.90, TPE £5.70, the airport £3) are Advance fares for one booked train, not the walk-up price. The Bee Network tap does not work on this train — buy a ticket. A ticket to \"Manchester CTLZ\" also covers the tram in zone 1, so the hop from Piccadilly to St Peter's Square or Victoria costs nothing more.\n\nThe tram is £4.60 because the airport is in zone 4 and the city center is zone 1 — one zone costs £1.40. Tap in at the platform and tap out at your stop with the same card; a missed tap-out is charged the full £4.60 and counts towards no cap. A same-day return by tap costs £4.90 in total under the all-zone off-peak day cap, or £7.10 if you tap in between 07:00 and 09:30 on a weekday. Nothing is sold on board; boarding untapped is £60 on the spot or a £120 penalty fare.\n\nThe taxi is metered: £3.40 to start by day, £3.80 between 22:00 and 06:00, then 20p per 123 metres by day or per 92.5 metres at night, plus 20p per passenger beyond the first and £1.80 for the airport pick-up barrier. Expect around £29.20 by day or £38.00 at night for the roughly 10-mile ride into the city center, before waiting time. No booking is needed at the ranks outside both terminals and The Station; cabs take five or six passengers, but the fare does not fall below a bus fare at any party size.\n\nThe bus 43 runs round the clock, so there is no true night gap. Trains leave the airport for Piccadilly at 00:38, 01:14, 01:50 and 02:28 on Tuesday to Saturday mornings, with the day's first at 03:21 (03:59 on Mondays) — the longest weekday hole is 53 minutes. The real rail gaps are Saturday night, last train 23:46 to Sunday's first at 07:37 (07:20 from 15 November), and Sunday night, 00:38 to 03:59.\n\nShow map\n\n**Can we count your visit?** We use Google Analytics to see which airports people look up. It sets cookies. Nothing runs until you say yes, and refusing changes nothing about the site. [What is stored](https://airporttocitycenter.com/cookies/) · [Privacy](https://airporttocitycenter.com/privacy/)\n\nRejectAcceptChoose\n\n## Cookie choices\n\n×\n\n**Strictly necessary**\n\nThis choice, kept on your device so we do not ask again. Nothing else.\n\nAlways on\n\n**Analytics**\n\nGoogle Analytics 4: which pages are read and roughly where from. Cookies `_ga` and `_ga_8DEX3DTMZW`, two years.\n\n**Partner links**\n\nTravelpayouts' script (emrldco.com, Hong Kong), which lets that network credit us when a booking follows a visit. It sets its own cookies, reads the page and any analytics identifier, and sends them outside the EU. It does not change the page. Off unless you tick it.\n\n[Full details](https://airporttocitycenter.com/cookies/)\n\nReject allSave choices",

      "summary": "This content provides information on various transport options from Manchester Airport to Manchester city center, specifically Piccadilly. Key options include:\n\n1. **Train to Piccadilly**: 13-20 minute ride, runs from 03:21 to 02:28, costs £6.20. Fastest option.\n2. **Metrolink Tram**: 49-60 minute ride, costs £4.60, operates from 05:45 to 23:15.\n3. **Bus 43**: 48-82 minute ride, costs £2, available 24/7 and makes multiple stops.\n4. **Pre-booked Private Transfer**: Approximately 15 minutes, £54, non-stop service.\n\nThe text also mentions pick-up locations, ticket purchase options, and provides links for booking and further details on transport schedules. It's highlighted that the train is the quickest while the bus is the cheapest."

year ago\n\nSave\n\nThere is the Bee Network app. Very handy. It's not so good for buying tram tickets for just a day but good for a weekly ticket.\n\nIt's much better for bus passes, you can buy a daily one for £5 or a 7 day for £20. Good value.\n\nThe system calculates where you have travelled and charges your card accordingly. You will pay the same as a day ticket.\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/17/ab/3e/fa/kwuk.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/KWUK?tab=forum)\n\n[KWUK](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/KWUK?tab=forum)\n\nGreater Manchester...\n\nDestination Expert\n\nfor Pollenca, Port de Pollenca\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/badge_forum_posts.png)33,869 posts\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/Appreciated.png)139 helpful votes\n\n1 year ago\n\nSave\n\nThe Bee Network app is for travel in the [Manchester](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Tourism-g187069-Manchester_Greater_Manchester_England-Vacations.html) area so you can't use it to book travel to [London](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Tourism-g186338-London_England-Vacations.html).\n\nCoaches are an alternative.\n\nThe most popular, I would say would be by train.\n\nYou can reduce the cost by committing to a particular timed train, called an Advance ticket and buying a railcard for £30 (soon to be £35) which will knock 33% off the price.\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/1a/f6/eb/a3/default-avatar-2020-38.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/ammarah2018?tab=forum)\n\n[Ammara H](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/ammarah2018?tab=forum)\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/Appreciated.png)2 helpful votes\n\n- [Forum](https://www.tripadvisor.com/ShowForum-g187069-i236-Manchester_Greater_Manchester_England.html)",

content\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/1a/f6/eb/a3/default-avatar-2020-38.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/stuka71?tab=forum)\n\n[briwire](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/stuka71?tab=forum)\n\nWarrington, United...\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/badge_forum_posts.png)3,206 posts\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/Appreciated.png)12 helpful votes\n\n2.\nRe: Public transport system\n\n1 year ago\n\nSave\n\nThere is the Bee Network app. That will show you maps of the Metrolink (trams) and bus system, showing live times and realtime locations of buses. Very handy. It's not so good for buying tram tickets for just a day but good for a weekly ticket.\n\nIt's much better for bus passes, you can buy a daily one for £5 or a 7 day for £20. Good value.\n\nWithout using the app, you can pay on the Metrolink, by either buying a paper ticket from a machine on a platform, or by simply tapping on at the start of your journey and tapping off at the end, using the yellow card readers on the platforms. The system calculates where you have travelled and charges your card accordingly. You will pay the same as a day ticket.\n\nReport inappropriate content\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/17/ab/3e/fa/kwuk.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/KWUK?tab=forum)\n\n[KWUK](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/KWUK?tab=forum)\n\nGreater Manchester...\n\nDestination Expert\n\nfor Pollenca, Port de Pollenca\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/badge_forum_posts.png)33,869 posts\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/Appreciated.png)139 helpful votes\n\n3.\nRe: Public transport system\n\n1 year ago\n\nSave\n\nAnd be aware of the Free Bus service on two circuitous routes within the city centre. Completely free to everyone, no tickets, nor passes, nor tapping of cards.\n\n[https://tfgm.com/ways-to-travel/bus/free-bus](https://www.tripadvisor.com/ExternalLinkInterstitial?redirectTo=https%3A%2F%2Ftfgm.com%2Fways-to-travel%2Fbus%2Ffree-bus)\n\nEdited: 1 year ago\n\nReport inappropriate content\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/03/00/b7/2f/muppetinmanchester.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/MuppetInManchester?tab=forum)\n\n[MuppetInManchester](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/MuppetInManchester?tab=forum)\n\nManchester, United...\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/badge_forum_posts.png)920 posts\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/Appreciated.png)4 helpful votes\n\n4.\nRe: Public transport system\n\n1 year ago\n\nSave\n\n@mysticmanc, could we have this as a pinned thread?\n\nReport inappropriate content\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/1a/f6/eb/a3/default-avatar-2020-38.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/ammarah2018?tab=forum)\n\n[Ammara H](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/ammarah2018?tab=forum)\n\nBekkestua, Norway\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/badge_forum_posts.png)112 posts\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/Appreciated.png)2 helpful votes\n\n5.\nRe: Public transport system\n\n1 year ago\n\nSave\n\nThankyou so much. This is really helpful.\n\nReport inappropriate content\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/1a/f6/eb/a3/default-avatar-2020-38.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/ammarah2018?tab=forum)\n\n[Ammara H](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/ammarah2018?tab=forum)\n\nBekkestua, Norway\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/badge_forum_posts.png)112 posts\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/Appreciated.png)2 helpful votes\n\n6.\nRe: Public transport system\n\n1 year ago\n\nSave\n\nThanks for your detailed reply. I have downloaded the app now. Do you know if children travel free with an adult or do we need to pay?\n\nCan I use the same app to buy tickets from [Manchester](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Tourism-g187069-Manchester_Greater_Manchester_England-Vacations.html) to [London](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Tourism-g186338-London_England-Vacations.html)?\n\nReport inappropriate content\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/1a/f6/e4/59/default-avatar-2020-49.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/andy4225?tab=forum)\n\n[Andy4225](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/andy4225?tab=forum)\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/badge_forum_posts.png)89 posts\n\n7.\nRe: Public transport system\n\n1 year ago\n\nSave\n\nPublic transport in the UK is nowhere near as co-ordinated as it is in most of [Europe](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Tourism-g4-Europe-Vacations.html). You can get day tickets that cover multi forms of local transport eg train, tram and buses but they generally aren't good value, it's usually much cheaper if you can stick to one form of transport eg tram or bus.\n\nYou have to pay for children unless very young (under 5). On the trams you can get family tickets which cover 2 adults and up to 3 children, you can buy from ticket machines on the platforms, don't think you can from the app. Last time I looked you can't buy any day tickets on the app.\n\nReport inappropriate content\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/1a/f6/eb/a3/default-avatar-2020-38.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/stuka71?tab=forum)\n\n[briwire](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/stuka71?tab=forum)\n\nWarrington, United...\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/badge_forum_posts.png)3,206 posts\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/Appreciated.png)12 helpful votes\n\n8.\nRe: Public transport system\n\n1 year ago\n\nSave\n\nThe Bee Network app is for travel in the [Manchester](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Tourism-g187069-Manchester_Greater_Manchester_England-Vacations.html) area so you can't use it to book travel to [London](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Tourism-g186338-London_England-Vacations.html).\n\nYou can [fly from](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Flights-g187069-Manchester_Greater_Manchester_England-Cheap_Discount_Airfares.html) Manchester airport to one of those around London, but that is offset by having to travel out to Manchester airport and into London at the other end. Adding a couple of hours to the journey.\n\nCoaches are an alternative. National Express and Flixbus are popular and probably the cheapest option. They have their own websites.\n\nThe most popular, I would say would be by train. They run from Manchester Piccadilly station to London Euston in as little as 2 hours for an express service. Very frequent service, which is operated by Avanti West Coast, you can book on their website or their app.\n\nYou can reduce the cost by committing to a particular timed train, called an Advance ticket and buying a railcard for £30 (soon to be £35) which will knock 33% off the price. If there are two of you, a Two Together railcard will cover both passengers for the same price. I'm seeing them for as little as £48 for two people with a railcard in a midweek day in a couple of weeks time.\n\nReport inappropriate content\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/1a/f6/eb/a3/default-avatar-2020-38.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/ammarah2018?tab=forum)\n\n[Ammara H](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/ammarah2018?tab=forum)\n\nBekkestua, Norway\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/badge_forum_posts.png)112 posts\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/Appreciated.png)2 helpful votes\n\n9.\nRe: Public transport system\n\n1 year ago\n\nSave\n\nThankyou for detailed information. Very helpful.\n\nReport inappropriate content\n\n[![](https://media-cdn.tripadvisor.com/media/photo-l/1a/f6/eb/a3/default-avatar-2020-38.jpg)](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/ammarah2018?tab=forum)\n\n[Ammara H](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Profile/ammarah2018?tab=forum)\n\nBekkestua, Norway\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/badge_forum_posts.png)112 posts\n\n![](https://static.tacdn.com/img2/badges/20px/Appreciated.png)2 helpful votes\n\n10.\nRe: Public transport system\n\n1 year ago\n\nSave\n\nSorry to bother you again, where can I buy railcard? I checked the price on avanti west coast for an adult and a child age 10, it asks for railcard option.\n\nReport inappropriate content\n\n1-10 _of_ 25 replies\n\nSorted by\n\nOldest firstNewest Please plan ahead.\n\n[Find out more](https://tfgm.com/tram-improvement-works/major-tram-works)\n\n## What can we help you with?\n\n## Explore\n\n![](https://tfgm.com/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.ctfassets.net%2Fs1msor4qyasd%2F5ufdpkLv3hJThoqdxo1kkX%2F5b612785eb7ab45245e5a03d6d6d28ee%2F23-0603-Metrolink-Engineering-Works-MPU-resized2.jpg&w=3840&q=75)\n\n### Plan Ahead for October Tram Disruption\n\nVisit our dedicated webpage to find out how these works could affect your journey.\n\n[Learn more](https://tfgm.com/tram-improvement-works/major-tram-works)\n\n![](https://tfgm.com/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.ctfassets.net%2Fs1msor4qyasd%2F5fRccLLYB4H3bMghbNcH9%2Ff5b42a1ffb60174cb3a20c550b9f5079%2Fwebsite_banners-01.jpg&w=3840&q=75)\n\n### Join the Voice of the Bee Network\n\nThe Voice of the Bee Network share their experiences and ideas to help improve the Bee Network.\n\n[Learn more](https://tfgm.com/consultations-and-engagement/voice-of-the-bee-network)\n\n![](https://tfgm.com/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.ctfassets.net%2Fs1msor4qyasd%2F2ZPfeqiJerCiaz1s04n2E2%2F1ef1b97fa1ca95381b643f2c3a69fdca%2F640x360.jpg&w=3840&q=75)\n\n### Buses. But better.\n\nMaking changes and improvements to your Bee Network.\n\n[Learn more](https://tfgm.com/ways-to-travel/bus/bus-network-development)\n\n[![Advert](https://tfgm.com/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.ctfassets.net%2Fs1msor4qyasd%2F67xwT39JvWpWDxaLSDBdxB%2F86fe66758f051d6844d74a2172c384b6%2FREPUBLIC_BeeNetwork_Digital-Ads_MPU300x250_V2.png&w=640&q=75)](https://republicofmayfield.com/)\n\n[![Advert](https://tfgm.com/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.ctfassets.net%2Fs1msor4qyasd%2FfEfzYPN38itUTLCEiSqo5%2F405b50d0ab612466cc602bb3cffd547f%2FMayfieldPark_BeeNetwork_Digital-Ads_Leaderboard728x90_v2.png&w=1920&q=75)](https://republicofmayfield.com/)",

This is lower than the [national cap of 3 GBP](https://bus-news.com/single-bus-fares-in-england-rise-to-3/).\n- Hopper Tickets: Single fares will allow passengers to transfer between Bee Network buses within one hour of purchase at no additional cost.\n- Day Ticket: 5 GBP for adults and 2.50 GBP for children.\n- Weekly Ticket: 20 GBP for adults and 10 GBP for children.\n- 28-Day Ticket: 80 GBP for adults and 40 GBP for children.\n- Annual Ticket: A new option priced at 2.20 GBP per day, offering savings and flexible payment options through local Credit Unions.\n\nIn March 2025, contactless payment options will launch, allowing passengers to tap in and out across buses and trams, ensuring they pay the best daily or weekly fare.\n\nA ticket for any bus + 1 tram zone will be priced at 6 GBP, any bus + 2 tram zones will cost 7.30 GBP, any bus + 3 tram zones will cost 8.70 GBP and any bus + all tram zones will be priced at 9.50 GBP.\n\nThis would allow for seamless travel across bus, tram, and train, with passengers benefiting from unified ticketing and capped fares.\n\nMayor of Greater Manchester, Andy Burnham, said:\n\nWe’re cutting the price of travel to get more people on board and putting an end to the complicated and disjointed tickets that people have previously struggled with.\n\n## We value your privacy\n✕\n\n## Cookie Preferences\n**Functional**\n\nSave My Preferences\n\n×\n\n### Sign in to continue\nPassword",

      "markdown": "Policy\n\n# Greater Manchester’s Bee Network Set to Introduce Simpler Fares\n\n### Author:\n\nTiana May\n\n### Published:\n\n2 Jan 2025\n\nGreater Manchester has confirmed details of its new, [streamlined fare structure](https://bus-news.com/greater-manchesters-bee-network-to-lower-bus-fares/) for Bee Network buses, coinciding with the final stage of bringing all buses under local control.\n\nFrom 5 January, the Bee Network will operate across the entire city region, marking a significant milestone in Greater Manchester’s plans for an integrated public transport system.\n\n![Bee Network buses in Greater Manchester](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\nBee Network buses in Greater Manchester\n\n© Transport for Greater Manchester\n\nThis move means bus travel will become more affordable and straightforward for passengers.\n\nKey changes to fares include:\n\n- Single Fares: Adults can travel for 2 GBP, and children for 1 GBP on any Bee Network bus. This is lower than the [national cap of 3 GBP](https://bus-news.com/single-bus-fares-in-england-rise-to-3/).\n- Hopper Tickets: Single fares will allow passengers to transfer between Bee Network buses within one hour of purchase at no additional cost.\n- Day Ticket: 5 GBP for adults and 2.50 GBP for children.\n- Weekly Ticket: 20 GBP for adults and 10 GBP for children.\n- 28-Day Ticket: 80 GBP for adults and 40 GBP for children.\n- Annual Ticket: A new option priced at 2.20 GBP per day, offering savings and flexible payment options through local Credit Unions.\n\nThe changes are made possible by Greater Manchester’s adoption of a franchising model, with a significant proportion of the fleet being new vehicles. By April, 66% of buses in the southern parts of the city-region will be new.\n\nThe Bee Network app is also being enhanced, enabling passengers to track buses and access real-time stop information. In March 2025, contactless payment options will launch, allowing passengers to tap in and out across buses and trams, ensuring they pay the best daily or weekly fare.\n\nWhat’s more, the introduction of capped fares will further simplify multimodal travel.\n\nA ticket for any bus + 1 tram zone will be priced at 6 GBP, any bus + 2 tram zones will cost 7.30 GBP, any bus + 3 tram zones will cost 8.70 GBP and any bus + all tram zones will be priced at 9.50 GBP.\n\nPlans are also underway to [integrate eight commuter rail lines into the Bee Network by 2028](https://railway-news.com/greater-manchester-to-integrate-rail-into-bee-network-by-2028/), with additional routes expected by 2030. This would allow for seamless travel across bus, tram, and train, with passengers benefiting from unified ticketing and capped fares.\n\n> Mayor of Greater Manchester, Andy Burnham, said:\n>\n> Our mission, when we set out to build the Bee Network, was to make getting around Greater Manchester easier, cheaper and more reliable. Doing so will mean we connect people to education, jobs and each other like never before. We’re sticking to that promise.\n>\n> We’re cutting the price of travel to get more people on board and putting an end to the complicated and disjointed tickets that people have previously struggled with.\n\n### Share\n\n[![Share on LinkedIn](https://bus-news.com/wp-content/themes/globala2bmedia/graphics/linkedin-share.svg)LinkedIn](https://www.linkedin.com/sharing/share-offsite/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fbus-news.com%2Fgreater-manchesters-bee-network-set-to-introduce-simpler-fares%2F)![Copy       "summary": "Greater Manchester has announced a new streamlined fare structure for its Bee Network buses, effective January 5, 2025. This marks the culmination of efforts to localize bus control and simplify public transport. Key changes include: single fares of £2 for adults and £1 for children, free transfers within one hour using Hopper Tickets, and various day, weekly, and annual ticket options. A new app will enhance user experience by providing real-time information and contactless payment will roll out in March 2025. Future plans include integrating commuter rail lines into the network by 2028, promoting unified ticketing and capped fares for multimodal transport."
    }
